     3: // symbols, relocations, and diagnostics. Two phases over an
     4: // AssemblyBuilder sized from the line count: assembly_source_parse turns
     5: // each statement into an AssemblyInstruction — labels, directives,
     6: // expressions, and operands resolved — and assembly_instructions_emit
     7: // sizes and encodes them, laying out symbols and relocations and
     8: // range-checking branches. Unknown or malformed input is a diagnostic,
     9: // never a guess.
    10: //
    11: // Instruction knowledge is table-driven, not hand-coded per mnemonic: x86
    12: // selection and encoding go through the generated metadata
    13: // (assembly_x86_metadata_select_source_form, x86_64_metadata.h), with the
    14: // assembly_x86_instruction_size/encode paths covering the legacy subset
    15: // and every prefix family up to EVEX/APX; AArch64 parsing walks the
    16: // generated syntax templates and semantic tables (aarch64_syntax.h,
    17: // aarch64_*_semantics.h) and encodes through aarch64_encoding.h.
    18: //
    19: // Layout, in file order; each anchor is a definition to search for:
    20: //   assembly_space .. assembly_symbol_intern       builder plumbing, labels,
    21: //                                                  symbol interning
    22: //   assembly_expression_parse,                     constant/symbol expression
    23: //   assembly_expression_merge                      evaluation
    24: //   assembly_register_parse,                       operand parsing for both
    25: //   assembly_x86_memory_parse_intel                dialects and both targets
    26: //   assembly_aarch64_*                             AArch64 operand forms,
    27: //                                                  system registers, SIMD
    28: //                                                  spellings, row selection
------
    29: //   assembly_x86_vector_form ..                    x86 form classification
    30: //   assembly_x86_instruction_size                  and instruction sizing/
    31: //                                                  encoding
    32: //   assembly_x86_metadata_*                        metadata-driven selection
    33: //                                                  and emission
    34: //   assembly_instruction_parse,                    statement recognition and
    35: //   assembly_source_parse                          the parse phase driver
    36: //   assembly_relocation_append,                    the emit phase and the
    37: //   assembly_instructions_emit, assembly_encode    public entry point
    38: 
    39: #include <buster/lib/compiler/assembly/assembly.h>
    40: #include <buster/lib/compiler/assembly/aarch64_encoding.h>
    41: #include <buster/lib/compiler/assembly/aarch64_direct_simd_semantics.h>
    42: #include <buster/lib/compiler/assembly/aarch64_control_semantics.h>
    43: #include <buster/lib/compiler/assembly/aarch64_system_semantics.h>

 10671: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL void assembly_x86_metadata_append_feature(String8* names, u32* count, u32 capacity, String8 feature)
 10672: {
 10673:     if (names && count && *count < capacity)
 10674:     {
 10675:         for (u32 index = 0; index < *count; index += 1)
 10676:         {
 10677:             if (assembly_word_equal(names[index], feature)) return;
 10678:         }
 10679:         names[(*count)++] = feature;
 10680:     }

 10691:         {
 10692:             BusterX86MetadataPhysicalOperand operand = operands[index];
 10693:             if (operand.kind != BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_OPERAND_REGISTER) continue;
 10694:             u16 width = 0;
 10695:             if (operand.reg.physical_class == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_XMM)
 10696:                 width = 128;
 10697:             else if (operand.reg.physical_class == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_YMM)
 10698:                 width = 256;
------
 10699:             else if (operand.reg.physical_class == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_ZMM)
 10700:                 width = 512;
 10701:             if (width > max_vector_width) max_vector_width = width;
 10702:         }
 10703:         if (!max_vector_width) return;
 10704:         assembly_x86_metadata_append_feature(names, count, capacity, S8("avx512f"));
 10705:         if (max_vector_width >= 512)
 10706:             assembly_x86_metadata_append_feature(names, count, capacity, S8("avx512dq"));
 10707:         else
 10708:             assembly_x86_metadata_append_feature(names, count, capacity, S8("avx512vl"));
 10709:     }
 10710: }
 10711: 
 10712: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL u8 assembly_x86_metadata_physical_class(AssemblyRegisterClass class)
 10713: {
 10714:     return class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_GPR       ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_GPR
 10715:            : class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_XMM     ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_XMM
 10716:            : class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_YMM     ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_YMM
 10717:            : class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_ZMM     ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_ZMM
 10718:            : class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_OPMASK  ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_MASK
 10719:            : class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_TILE    ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_TMM
------
 10720:            : class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_MMX     ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_MMX
 10721:            : class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_X87     ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_SPECIAL
------
 10722:            : class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_BND     ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_BND
 10723:            : class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_CONTROL ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_CONTROL
------
 10724:            : class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_DEBUG   ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_DEBUG
 10725:            : class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_SEGMENT ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_SEGMENT
 10726:            : class == ASSEMBLY_REGISTER_SPECIAL ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_SPECIAL
 10727:                                                 : BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_UNKNOWN;
------
 10728: }
 10729: 
 10730: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL String8 assembly_x86_metadata_symbol_name(AssemblyBuilder* builder, u32 symbol)
 10731: {
 10732:     return symbol < builder->result.symbol_count ? builder->result.symbols[symbol].name : (String8){0};
 10733: }
 10734: 
 10735: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_physical_operand(AssemblyBuilder* builder, AssemblyOperand operand, bool relative,
 10736:                                                                  bool absolute, BusterX86MetadataPhysicalOperand* result)
 10737: {
 10738:     if (!result)
 10739:     {
 10740:         return false;
 10741:     }
 10742:     *result = (BusterX86MetadataPhysicalOperand){0};
 10743:     if (operand.kind == ASSEMBLY_OPERAND_REGISTER)
 10744:     {
 10745:         result->kind = BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_OPERAND_REGISTER;
------
 10746:         result->width = operand.reg.width;
 10747:         result->reg = (BusterX86MetadataPhysicalRegister){
 10748:             .index = operand.reg.index,
 10749:             .width = operand.reg.width,
 10750:             .physical_class = assembly_x86_metadata_physical_class(operand.reg.class),
------
 10751:             .high_byte = operand.reg.high_byte,
 10752:         };
 10753:         return result->reg.physical_class != BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_CLASS_UNKNOWN;
 10754:     }
 10755:     if (operand.kind == ASSEMBLY_OPERAND_MEMORY)
 10756:     {
 10757:         AssemblyMemory memory = operand.memory;
 10758:         result->kind = BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_OPERAND_MEMORY;
 10759:         // Metadata memory widths describe the encoded scalar element for
 10760:         // vector forms, while the source qualifier records the aggregate
 10761:         // vector width (xmmword/ymmword/zmmword).  Leave aggregate widths
 10762:         // unresolved so the selected vector form supplies the element width;
 10763:         // scalar and branch memory widths retain their source value.
 10764:         result->width = (memory.width > 64 && memory.width != 80) ? 0 : memory.width ? memory.width : (relative ? 64 : 0);
 10765:         // Width 80 is an x87 scalar element, not an aggregate vector
------
 10773:         result->memory.has_segment = memory.has_segment;
 10774:         result->memory.has_base = memory.has_base;
 10775:         result->memory.has_index = memory.has_index;
 10776:         result->memory.rip_relative = memory.rip_relative;
 10777:         result->memory.vsib = memory.vsib;
 10778:         if (memory.has_base)
 10779:         {
 10780:             result->memory.base = (BusterX86MetadataPhysicalRegister){
 10781:                 .index = memory.base.index,
 10782:                 .width = memory.base.width,
 10783:                 .physical_class = assembly_x86_metadata_physical_class(memory.base.class),
 10784:             };
 10785:         }
 10786:         if (memory.has_index)
 10787:         {
 10788:             result->memory.index = (BusterX86MetadataPhysicalRegister){
 10789:                 .index = memory.index.index,
 10790:                 .width = memory.index.width,
 10791:                 .physical_class = assembly_x86_metadata_physical_class(memory.index.class),
 10792:             };
 10793:         }
 10794:         if (memory.displacement.has_symbol)
 10795:         {
 10796:             result->memory.symbol = assembly_x86_metadata_symbol_name(builder, memory.displacement.symbol);
 10797:             result->memory.has_symbol = result->memory.symbol.length != 0;
 10798:             result->memory.addend = memory.displacement.addend;
------
 10799:             if (!result->memory.has_symbol)
 10800:             {
 10801:                 return false;
 10802:             }
 10803:         }
 10804:         else
 10805:         {
 10806:             if (memory.displacement.has_unsigned_addend)
------
 10807:             {
 10808:                 return false;
 10809:             }
 10810:             result->memory.displacement = memory.displacement.addend;
 10811:             result->memory.has_displacement = memory.displacement.addend != 0;
------
 10832:     if (operand.kind != ASSEMBLY_OPERAND_EXPRESSION)
 10833:     {
 10834:         return false;
 10835:     }
 10836:     result->kind = absolute       ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_OPERAND_ABSOLUTE
 10837:                    : relative    ? BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_OPERAND_RELATIVE
 10838:                                  : BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_OPERAND_IMMEDIATE;
 10839:     result->width = 0;
 10840:     if (operand.expression.has_symbol)
 10841:     {
 10842:         result->symbol = assembly_x86_metadata_symbol_name(builder, operand.expression.symbol);
 10843:         result->has_symbol = result->symbol.length != 0;
 10844:         result->addend = operand.expression.addend;
 10845:         return result->has_symbol;
 10846:     }
 10847:     if (operand.expression.has_unsigned_addend)
 10848:     {
 10849:         result->unsigned_value = operand.expression.unsigned_addend;
 10850:         result->has_unsigned_value = true;
 10851:     }
 10852:     else
 10853:     {
 10854:         result->value = operand.expression.addend;
 10855:         result->has_value = true;
 10856:     }
 10857:     return true;
------
 10858: }
 10859: 
 10860: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_absolute_mnemonic(String8 mnemonic)
 10861: {
 10862:     return assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("jmpabs"));
 10863: }
 10864: 
 10865: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_adjust_relative_literals(BusterX86MetadataPhysicalOperand* operands, u32 operand_count,
 10866:                                                                           u64 offset, u32 byte_count)
 10867: {
 10868:     if (!operands)
 10869:     {
 10870:         return false;
 10871:     }
 10872:     bool has_relative_literal = false;
 10873:     for (u32 index = 0; index < operand_count; index += 1)
 10874:     {
 10875:         has_relative_literal |= operands[index].kind == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_OPERAND_RELATIVE && !operands[index].has_symbol;
------
 10876:     }
 10877:     if (has_relative_literal)
 10878:     {
 10879:         if (offset > (u64)INT64_MAX || (u64)byte_count > (u64)INT64_MAX - offset)
 10880:         {
------
 10902:             {
 10903:                 return false;
 10904:             }
 10905:             operand->value -= next;
 10906:         }
 10907:     }
 10908: 
 10909:     return true;
 10910: }
 10911: 
 10912: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_relative_mnemonic(String8 mnemonic)
 10913: {
 10914:     if (!mnemonic.length)
 10915:     {
 10916:         return false;
 10917:     }
 10918:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("jmpabs")))
 10919:     {
 10920:         return false;
 10921:     }
 10922:     if (mnemonic.pointer[0] == 'j' || mnemonic.pointer[0] == 'J')
 10923:     {
 10924:         return true;
 10925:     }
 10926:     return assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("call")) || assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("call_near")) ||
 10927:            assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("loop")) ||
------
 10928:            assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("loope")) || assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("loopz")) ||
 10929:            assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("loopne")) || assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("loopnz")) ||
 10930:            assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("xbegin"));
 10931: }
 10932: 
 10933: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL String8 assembly_x86_metadata_att_string_alias(AssemblySyntax syntax, String8 mnemonic)
 10934: {
 10935:     if (syntax == ASSEMBLY_SYNTAX_ATT)
 10936:     {
 10937:         if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("movsl"))) return S8("movsd");
 10938:         if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("cmpsl"))) return S8("cmpsd");
 10939:         if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("stosl"))) return S8("stosd");
 10940:         if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("lodsl"))) return S8("lodsd");
 10941:         if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("scasl"))) return S8("scasd");
 10942:         if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("insl"))) return S8("insd");
 10943:         if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("outsl"))) return S8("outsd");
 10944:     }
 10945: 
 10946:     return mnemonic;
 10947: }
 10948: 
------
 10949: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_string_has_token(BusterX86MetadataString string, String8 token)
 10950: {
 10951:     u32 offset = 0;
 10952:     while (offset < string.length)
 10953:     {
 10954:         while (offset < string.length && assembly_space(buster_x86_metadata_string_byte(string, offset))) offset += 1;
 10955:         u32 start = offset;
 10956:         while (offset < string.length && !assembly_space(buster_x86_metadata_string_byte(string, offset))) offset += 1;
 10957:         u32 length = offset - start;
 10958:         if (length != token.length) continue;
 10959:         bool equal = true;
 10960:         for (u32 index = 0; index < length; index += 1)
 10961:         {
 10962:             if (assembly_ascii_lower(buster_x86_metadata_string_byte(string, start + index)) !=
 10963:                 assembly_ascii_lower(token.pointer[index]))
 10964:             {
------
 10965:                 equal = false;
 10966:                 break;
 10967:             }
 10968:         }
 10969:         if (equal) return true;
 10970:     }
 10971:     return false;
 10972: }
 10973: 
 10974: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_mnemonic_has_att_operand_order_exception(String8 mnemonic)
 10975: {
 10976:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("enter")))
 10977:     {
 10978:         BusterX86MetadataCandidateRange candidates = buster_x86_metadata_lookup_mnemonic(mnemonic);
 10979:         for (u32 position = 0; position < candidates.count; position += 1)
 10980:         {
 10981:             u32 form_id = 0;
 10982:             BusterX86MetadataForm form = {0};
 10983:             if (buster_x86_metadata_candidate_at(candidates, position, &form_id) && buster_x86_metadata_form(form_id, &form) &&
 10984:                 assembly_x86_metadata_string_has_token(form.attributes, S8("ATT_OPERAND_ORDER_EXCEPTION")))
 10985:                 return true;
 10986:         }
 10987:     }
 10988: 
 10989:     return false;
------
 10990: }
 10991: 
 10992: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL String8 assembly_x86_metadata_mnemonic(String8 mnemonic)
 10993: {
 10994:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("loopz"))) return S8("loope");
 10995:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("loopnz"))) return S8("loopne");
 10996:     // GNU/AT&T spellings for the accumulator sign-extension family are
 10997:     // aliases of the Intel mnemonics represented by metadata.
 10998:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("cbtw"))) return S8("cbw");
 10999:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("cwtl"))) return S8("cwde");
------
 11000:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("cltq"))) return S8("cdqe");
 11001:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("cwtd"))) return S8("cwd");
 11002:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("cltd"))) return S8("cdq");
 11003:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("cqto"))) return S8("cqo");
 11004:     // XED's condition-family spellings use Z/NZ and the negative aliases
 11005:     // (NLE, etc.) as canonical keys.  The handwritten front end accepts the
 11006:     // shorter Intel aliases (JE, SETNE, SETG, CMOVE); normalize those keys
 11007:     // before metadata candidate selection.
------
 11031:             if (buster_x86_metadata_lookup_mnemonic(base).count)
 11032:             {
 11033:                 return base;
 11034:             }
 11035:         }
 11036:     }
 11037: 
 11038:     return mnemonic;
 11039: }
 11040: 
 11041: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL u16 assembly_x86_metadata_att_scalar_extend_source_width(String8 mnemonic)
 11042: {
 11043:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("movzbw")) || assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("movzbl")) ||
 11044:         assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("movzbq")) || assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("movsbw")) ||
 11045:         assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("movsbl")) || assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("movsbq")))
 11046:         return 8;
 11047:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("movzwl")) || assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("movzwq")) ||
 11048:         assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("movswl")) || assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("movswq")))
 11049:         return 16;
 11050:     if (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("movslq"))) return 32;
 11051:     return 0;
 11052: }
 11053: 
 11054: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_apx_disasm_alias(
 11055:     String8 mnemonic, BusterX86MetadataPhysicalAttributes* attributes)
 11056: {
------
 11057:     if (!mnemonic.length || !attributes)
 11058:     {
 11059:         return false;
 11060:     }
 11061:     BusterX86MetadataCandidateRange candidates = buster_x86_metadata_lookup_mnemonic(mnemonic);
 11062:     bool found = false;
 11063:     bool ambiguous = false;
 11064:     for (u32 position = 0; position < candidates.count; position += 1)
 11065:     {
 11066:         u32 form_id = 0;
 11067:         BusterX86MetadataForm form = {0};
 11068:         if (!buster_x86_metadata_candidate_at(candidates, position, &form_id) || !buster_x86_metadata_form(form_id, &form))
 11069:         {
------
 11079:     if (found && !ambiguous)
 11080:     {
 11081:         // The generated disassembly aliases (IMULZU and SET*ZU) select the
 11082:         // APX NDD family.  An explicit {nf} remains an independent source
 11083:         // attribute, so IMULZU can select either its NF=0 or NF=1 row.
 11084:         attributes->apx_flags |= BUSTER_X86_METADATA_APX_NDD;
 11085:     }
 11086:     return found && !ambiguous;
 11087: }
 11088: 
 11089: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_mnemonic_requires_dfv(String8 mnemonic)
 11090: {
 11091:     BusterX86MetadataCandidateRange candidates = buster_x86_metadata_lookup_mnemonic(mnemonic);
 11092:     for (u32 position = 0; position < candidates.count; position += 1)
 11093:     {
 11094:         u32 form_id = 0;
 11095:         if (buster_x86_metadata_candidate_at(candidates, position, &form_id) &&
 11096:             buster_x86_metadata_form_requires_dfv(form_id))
 11097:         {
 11098:             return true;
 11099:         }
 11100:     }
 11101:     return false;
 11102: }
 11103: 
 11104: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_parse_dfv(AssemblyOperand operand, u8* value)
------
 11105: {
 11106:     if (operand.kind != ASSEMBLY_OPERAND_EXPRESSION || operand.expression.has_symbol)
 11107:     {
 11108:         return false;
 11109:     }
 11110:     if (operand.expression.has_unsigned_addend)
 11111:     {
 11112:         if (operand.expression.unsigned_addend > 15) return false;
 11113:         if (value) *value = (u8)operand.expression.unsigned_addend;
 11114:         return true;
 11115:     }
 11116:     if (operand.expression.addend < 0 || operand.expression.addend > 15)
 11117:     {
 11118:         return false;
 11119:     }
------
 11120:     if (value) *value = (u8)operand.expression.addend;
 11121:     return true;
 11122: }
 11123: 
 11124: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_mnemonic_has_visible_operands(String8 mnemonic)
 11125: {
 11126:     BusterX86MetadataCandidateRange candidates = buster_x86_metadata_lookup_mnemonic(mnemonic);
 11127:     for (u32 position = 0; position < candidates.count; position += 1)
 11128:     {
 11129:         u32 form_id = 0;
 11130:         BusterX86MetadataForm form = {0};
 11131:         if (!buster_x86_metadata_candidate_at(candidates, position, &form_id) || !buster_x86_metadata_form(form_id, &form))
 11132:         {
 11133:             continue;
 11134:         }
 11135:         for (u32 operand_index = 0; operand_index < form.operand_count; operand_index += 1)
 11136:         {
 11137:             BusterX86MetadataOperand operand = {0};
 11138:             if (buster_x86_metadata_operand(form_id, operand_index, &operand) && operand.visible)
 11139:             {
------
 11140:                 return true;
 11141:             }
 11142:         }
 11143:     }
 11144:     return false;
 11145: }
 11146: 
 11147: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_suffix_alias(Target target, AssemblySyntax syntax, String8 mnemonic,
 11148:                                                              String8* base, AssemblyInstructionInfo* base_info, u8* width)
 11149: {
 11150:     if (syntax != ASSEMBLY_SYNTAX_ATT || mnemonic.length <= 1 || !base || !width)
 11151:     {
 11152:         return false;
 11153:     }
 11154:     char8 suffix = assembly_ascii_lower(mnemonic.pointer[mnemonic.length - 1]);
 11155:     u8 suffix_width = suffix == 'b' ? 8 : suffix == 'w' ? 16 : suffix == 'l' ? 32 : suffix == 'q' ? 64
 11156:                      : suffix == 's' ? 32
 11157:                      : suffix == 't' ? 80
 11158:                                       : 0;
 11159:     if (!suffix_width)
 11160:     {
 11161:         return false;
 11162:     }
------
 11206:         if (suffix == 'l' &&
 11207:             (info.opcode == ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_FLD || info.opcode == ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_FST ||
 11208:              info.opcode == ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_FSTP ||
 11209:              (info.opcode >= ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_FADD && info.opcode <= ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_FDIVR) ||
 11210:              (info.opcode >= ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_FCOM && info.opcode <= ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_FCMOVCC)))
 11211:         {
 11212:             suffix_width = 64;
 11213:         }
 11214:     }
 11215:     else if (!buster_x86_metadata_lookup_mnemonic(candidate).count ||
 11216:              !assembly_x86_metadata_mnemonic_has_visible_operands(candidate))
 11217:     {
 11218:         return false;
 11219:     }
 11220:     if (assembly_x86_metadata_relative_mnemonic(candidate) && !assembly_word_equal(candidate, S8("jmp")) &&
 11221:         !assembly_word_equal(candidate, S8("call")) && !assembly_word_equal(candidate, S8("call_near")))
 11222:     {
 11223:         return false;
 11224:     }
 11225:     *base = candidate;
 11226:     if (base_info)
 11227:     {
 11228:         *base_info = info;
 11229:     }
 11230:     *width = suffix_width;
 11231:     return true;
------
 11232: }
 11233: 
 11234: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_suffix_applies(AssemblyOpcode opcode, u32 operand_index, u32 operand_count)
 11235: {
------
 11236:     if (opcode == ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_LEA || assembly_x86_opcode_is_rotate(opcode) ||
 11237:         opcode == ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_MOVZX || opcode == ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_MOVSX || opcode == ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_MOVSXD)
 11238:     {
 11239:         return operand_index == 0;
 11240:     }
 11241:     if (opcode == ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_SHLD || opcode == ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_SHRD)
 11242:     {
 11243:         return operand_index < 2;
 11244:     }
 11245:     if (assembly_x86_opcode_is_shift(opcode))
 11246:     {
 11247:         // An explicit literal one is normalized away before metadata
 11248:         // selection.  In that ONE() topology the remaining data operand is
 11249:         // still the suffix-described operand.
------
 11250:         return operand_count == 1 ? operand_index == 0 : operand_index + 1 < operand_count;
 11251:     }
 11252:     return true;
 11253: }
 11254: 
 11255: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_suffix_width_matches(AssemblyInstructionInfo info, u8 suffix_width,
 11256:                                                                       AssemblyOperand const* operands,
 11257:                                                                       BusterX86MetadataPhysicalOperand* physical,
 11258:                                                                       u32 operand_count)
 11259: {
 11260:     bool saw_width_operand = false;
 11261:     for (u32 operand_index = 0; operand_index < operand_count; operand_index += 1)
 11262:     {
 11263:         if (!assembly_x86_metadata_suffix_applies(info.opcode, operand_index, operand_count))
 11264:         {
 11265:             continue;
 11266:         }
 11267:         AssemblyOperand operand = operands[operand_index];
 11268:         if (operand.kind == ASSEMBLY_OPERAND_REGISTER && operand.reg.width != suffix_width)
 11269:         {
 11270:             return false;
------
 11271:         }
 11272:         if (operand.kind == ASSEMBLY_OPERAND_REGISTER)
 11273:         {
 11274:             saw_width_operand = true;
 11275:         }
 11276:         if (operand.kind == ASSEMBLY_OPERAND_MEMORY)
 11277:         {
 11278:             if (operand.memory.width && operand.memory.width != suffix_width)

 11286:     // A suffix must describe a data-width operand.  This excludes metadata
 11287:     // mnemonics whose visible operand is only an immediate or branch target
 11288:     // (for example intb), while retaining register and memory aliases such as
 11289:     // smswl and pushpq.  PUSH's stack width is implicit, so the immediate-only
 11290:     // pushq spelling is the one valid suffix alias in 64-bit mode.
 11291:     return saw_width_operand ||
 11292:            (info.opcode == ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_PUSH && suffix_width == 64 && operand_count == 1 &&
 11293:             operands[0].kind == ASSEMBLY_OPERAND_EXPRESSION);

 11372: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_operand_decorators(BusterX86MetadataPhysicalAttributes* attributes,
 11373:                                                                     AssemblyOperand const* operands, u32 operand_count)

 11379:         {
 11380:             return false;

 11383:         {
 11384:             return false;
 11385:         }

 11387:         {

 11431:     return true;

 11456: 
 11457: // A typed EVEX decorator is source-level evidence that the metadata candidate
 11458: // is the intended instruction shape.  The handwritten parser can still report
 11459: // INVALID_OPERANDS for canonical census witnesses that deliberately reuse
 11460: // registers, and the feature-selection path historically downgraded a
 11461: // feature failure for the same reason.  Keep those legacy fallbacks for
 11462: // ordinary syntax, but let this metadata-proven semantic shape remain
 11463: // authoritative.  The predicate deliberately accepts maskless standalone SAE
 11464: // forms as well as MASKmskw forms; the form/attribute contract, not a mnemonic
 11465: // or census identity, determines eligibility.

 11467:     BusterX86MetadataForm form, BusterX86MetadataPhysicalQuery query)
 11468: {
 11469:     return buster_x86_metadata_typed_decorator_authoritative(form, query);

 11474: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL BusterX86MetadataEncodeStatus assembly_x86_metadata_instruction_parse(
 11475:     AssemblyBuilder* builder, String8 statement, u32 line, u32 column, u64 offset, Target target, AssemblySyntax syntax)
 11476: {
 11477:     builder->metadata_failure_typed_decorator_authoritative = false;

 11479:     {
 11480:         return BUSTER_X86_METADATA_ENCODE_UNKNOWN_MNEMONIC;
 11481:     }

 11482:     // FINIT/FCLEX and the FST* control-store aliases are the architectural
 11483:     // WAIT-prefixed aliases of metadata-native FN* forms.  Expand each source
 11484:     // instruction into two checked metadata instructions so the 0x9B wait
 11485:     // prefix remains metadata-produced rather than resurrecting a handwritten
 11486:     // opcode path.  The latter aliases retain their operands while replacing
 11487:     // only the mnemonic (for example `fstcw (%rax)` -> `fnstcw (%rax)`).

 11495:     else if (assembly_word_equal(first_word, S8("fstcw"))) wait_alias = S8("fnstcw");
 11496:     else if (assembly_word_equal(first_word, S8("fstenv"))) wait_alias = S8("fnstenv");
 11497:     else if (assembly_word_equal(first_word, S8("fsave"))) wait_alias = S8("fnsave");

 12494:     for (u32 index = 0; index < operand_count; index += 1)
 12495:     {
 12496:         has_immediate_operand |= physical[index].kind == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_OPERAND_IMMEDIATE;
 12497:     }
 12498:     // The three-operand APX spelling is NDD for arithmetic/logical forms.
 12499:     // IMUL's three-operand immediate form is the NF-capable legacy/APX row,
 12500:     // not NDD; leave it to the immediate-form selector below.
 12501:     if (operand_count == 3 &&

 12503:          assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("sub")) || assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("sbb")) ||
 12504:          assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("and")) || assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("or")) ||
 12505:          assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("xor")) ||
 12506:          (assembly_word_equal(mnemonic, S8("imul")) && !has_immediate_operand)))
 12507:     {

 12550:                 physical[index].has_value = false;
 12551:             }
 12552:         }
 12553:     }
 12554:     if (leading_sae)
 12555:     {
 12556:         attributes.sae = true;
 12557:         attributes.decorator_flags |= BUSTER_X86_METADATA_DECORATOR_SAE;
 12558:     }
 12559:     if (!assembly_x86_metadata_operand_decorators(&attributes, operands, operand_count))
 12560:     {
 12561:         return BUSTER_X86_METADATA_ENCODE_DECORATOR;
 12562:     }

 12568:     }

 12572:                                                 operand_count);

 12574:     // the assembler's baseline MMX contract accepts them without SSE2.  The

 12575:     // physical query is already restricted to MMX rows above, so supplying
 12576:     // the feature token here cannot make an XMM form pass the selector.

 12581:     }

 12629:     };

 12631:     u32 trial_count = relative_literal ? 15 : 1;

 12637:         {

 12639:         }

 12644:             &trial_mnemonic);

 12646:             (!relative_literal || trial_selection.selected_byte_count == trial_byte_count))

 12740:         {

 12743:     }

 12747: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_instruction_is_novel(AssemblyInstruction instruction)
 12748: {
 12749:     if (assembly_word_equal(instruction.metadata_mnemonic, S8("vcvtbf42hf8")) ||
 12750:         assembly_word_equal(instruction.metadata_mnemonic, S8("vpslld")) ||
 12751:         assembly_word_equal(instruction.metadata_mnemonic, S8("vpsrld")))
 12752:     {
 12753:         return true;
 12754:     }
 12755:     // PUSH immediate is a classic encoding that has no handwritten form yet.
 12756:     // Preserve its metadata result instead of restoring the handwritten
 12757:     // invalid-operand diagnostic just because the encoding is not an ISA
 12758:     // extension.  Metadata selects the signed-imm8 or sign-extended imm32
 12759:     // form and carries symbol relocations through to emission.
 12760:     if (assembly_word_equal(instruction.metadata_mnemonic, S8("PUSH")) && instruction.metadata_operand_count == 1 &&
 12761:         instruction.metadata_operands[0].kind == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_OPERAND_IMMEDIATE)
 12762:     {
------
 12931:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_TZCNT:
 12932:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_BSWAP:
 12933:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_BT:
 12934:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_BTC:
 12935:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_BTR:
 12936:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_BTS:
 12937:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_XCHG:
 12938:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_XADD:
 12939:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_CMPXCHG:
 12940:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_CMPXCHG8B:
 12941:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_CMPXCHG16B:
 12942:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_JCC:
 12943:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_SETCC:
 12944:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_CMOVCC:
 12945:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_MOVAPS:
 12946:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_MOVUPS:
 12947:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_MOVAPD:
 12948:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_MOVUPD:
 12949:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_MOVDQA:
 12950:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_MOVDQU:
 12951:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_XORPS:
 12952:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_XORPD:
 12953:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_PXOR:
 12954:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_ADDPS:
 12955:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_ADDPD:
 12956:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_ADDSS:
------
 13035:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_VPCMPUD:
 13036:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_VPCMPUQ:
 13037:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_VRNDSCALEPS:
 13038:     case ASSEMBLY_OPCODE_X86_VRNDSCALEPD:
 13039:         return true;
 13040:     default:
 13041:         return false;
 13042:     }
 13043: }

 13045: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_instruction_has_duplicate_registers(AssemblyInstruction instruction)
 13046: {
 13047:     for (u32 left = 0; left < instruction.metadata_operand_count; left += 1)
 13048:     {
 13049:         BusterX86MetadataPhysicalOperand left_operand = instruction.metadata_operands[left];
 13050:         if (left_operand.kind != BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_OPERAND_REGISTER)
 13051:         {
 13052:             continue;
 13053:         }
 13054:         for (u32 right = left + 1; right < instruction.metadata_operand_count; right += 1)
 13055:         {
 13056:             BusterX86MetadataPhysicalOperand right_operand = instruction.metadata_operands[right];
 13057:             if (right_operand.kind == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PHYSICAL_OPERAND_REGISTER &&
 13058:                 left_operand.reg.physical_class == right_operand.reg.physical_class &&
 13059:                 left_operand.reg.index == right_operand.reg.index && left_operand.reg.width == right_operand.reg.width)
 13060:             {
------
 13106:         }
 13107:         if (end < statement.length && assembly_word_equal(assembly_trim(string_slice(statement, index + 1, end)), S8("nf")))
 13108:         {
 13109:             return true;
 13110:         }
 13111:         index = end;
 13112:     }
 13113:     return false;
 13114: }

 13116: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL void assembly_x86_metadata_diagnostic(AssemblyBuilder* builder, BusterX86MetadataEncodeStatus status,
 13117:                                                            u32 line, u32 column, u32 length)
 13118: {
 13119:     AssemblyDiagnosticKind kind = ASSEMBLY_DIAGNOSTIC_INVALID_OPERANDS;
 13120:     String8 message = S8("metadata instruction form is not encodable");
 13121:     if (status == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_ENCODE_UNKNOWN_MNEMONIC)
 13122:     {
 13123:         kind = ASSEMBLY_DIAGNOSTIC_UNKNOWN_INSTRUCTION;
 13124:         message = S8("unknown instruction");
 13125:     }
 13126:     else if (status == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_ENCODE_INVALID_EXPRESSION)
 13127:     {
 13128:         kind = ASSEMBLY_DIAGNOSTIC_INVALID_EXPRESSION;
 13129:         message = S8("invalid instruction expression");
 13130:     }
 13131:     else if (status == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_ENCODE_FEATURE_MODE_PRIVILEGE)
------
 13186:         }
 13187:         bool fallback_allowed = target.cpu_arch == CPU_ARCH_X86_64 &&
 13188:                                  (!handwritten_succeeded || metadata_authoritative || unsigned_expression || handwritten_requires_metadata);
 13189:         if (fallback_allowed)
 13190:         {
 13191:             builder->instruction_count = instruction_count;
 13192:             builder->result.symbol_count = symbol_count;
 13193:             builder->result.relocation_count = relocation_count;
 13194:             builder->result.diagnostic_count = diagnostic_count;
 13195:             builder->output_count = output_count;
 13196:             BusterX86MetadataEncodeStatus status = assembly_x86_metadata_instruction_parse(builder, statement, line, column, offset, target, syntax);
 13197:             if (status == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_ENCODE_SUCCESS)
 13198:             {
 13199:                 if (metadata_authoritative)
 13200:                 {
 13201:                     return;
 13202:                 }
 13203:                 bool metadata_novel = builder->instruction_count > instruction_count &&
 13204:                                       assembly_x86_metadata_instruction_is_novel(builder->instructions[instruction_count]);
 13205:                 bool metadata_source_authoritative = false;
 13206:                 if (builder->instruction_count > instruction_count)
 13207:                 {
 13208:                     AssemblyInstruction metadata_instruction = builder->instructions[instruction_count];
 13209:                     BusterX86MetadataForm metadata_form = {0};
 13210:                     if (buster_x86_metadata_form(metadata_instruction.metadata_form_id, &metadata_form))
 13211:                     {
------
 13212:                         BusterX86MetadataPhysicalQuery metadata_query = {
 13213:                             .operands = metadata_instruction.metadata_operands,
 13214:                             .operand_count = metadata_instruction.metadata_operand_count,
 13215:                             .attributes = metadata_instruction.metadata_attributes,
 13216:                             .address_size = metadata_instruction.metadata_address_size,
 13217:                             .execution_mode = BUSTER_X86_METADATA_EXECUTION_MODE_64,
 13218:                             .source_semantics = true,
 13219:                         };
------
 13220:                         metadata_source_authoritative = assembly_x86_metadata_typed_decorator_authoritative(metadata_form, metadata_query) ||
 13221:                                                         buster_x86_metadata_legacy_xmm_memory_authoritative(metadata_form, metadata_query) ||
 13222:                                                         buster_x86_metadata_block_memory_source_authoritative(metadata_form, metadata_query);
 13223:                     }
 13224:                 }
 13225:                 if ((handwritten_kind == ASSEMBLY_DIAGNOSTIC_UNSUPPORTED_FEATURE && !metadata_source_authoritative) ||
 13226:                     (handwritten_kind == ASSEMBLY_DIAGNOSTIC_INVALID_OPERANDS &&
 13227:                      (!metadata_source_authoritative &&
 13228:                       (!metadata_novel || assembly_x86_metadata_instruction_has_duplicate_registers(builder->instructions[instruction_count])))))
 13229:                 {
 13230:                     builder->instruction_count = instruction_count;
 13231:                     builder->result.symbol_count = symbol_count;
 13232:                     builder->result.relocation_count = relocation_count;
 13233:                     builder->result.diagnostic_count = diagnostic_count;
 13234:                     builder->output_count = output_count;
 13235:                     if (has_handwritten_diagnostic)
------
 13236:                     {
 13237:                         assembly_diagnostic(builder, handwritten_diagnostic.kind, handwritten_diagnostic.line, handwritten_diagnostic.column,
 13238:                                             handwritten_diagnostic.length, handwritten_diagnostic.message);
 13239:                     }
 13240:                 }
 13241:                 return;
 13242:             }
 13243:             if (metadata_authoritative)
------
 13244:             {
 13245:                 // Once a migrated instruction has entered metadata selection, do not
 13246:                 // silently resurrect handwritten bytes on a metadata failure.  Restore
 13247:                 // the pre-parse state and surface the checked producer's diagnostic.
 13248:                 builder->instruction_count = instruction_count;
 13249:                 builder->result.symbol_count = symbol_count;
 13250:                 builder->result.relocation_count = relocation_count;
 13251:                 builder->result.diagnostic_count = diagnostic_count;
 13252:                 builder->output_count = output_count;
 13253:                 u32 length = statement.length > UINT32_MAX ? UINT32_MAX : (u32)statement.length;
 13254:                 assembly_x86_metadata_diagnostic(builder, status, line, column, length);
 13255:                 return;
 13256:             }
 13257:             builder->instruction_count = instruction_count;
 13258:             builder->result.symbol_count = symbol_count;
 13259:             builder->result.relocation_count = relocation_count;
 13260:             builder->result.diagnostic_count = diagnostic_count;
 13261:             builder->output_count = output_count;
 13262:             bool metadata_precise = handwritten_requires_metadata || (!handwritten_succeeded &&
 13263:                                     (handwritten_kind != ASSEMBLY_DIAGNOSTIC_INVALID_OPERANDS ||
 13264:                                      (status == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_ENCODE_FEATURE_MODE_PRIVILEGE &&
 13265:                                       (builder->metadata_failure_typed_decorator_authoritative ||
 13266:                                        (assembly_x86_statement_has_extended_gpr(statement) &&
 13267:                                         !assembly_x86_statement_has_no_flags_decorator(statement)))) ||
 13268:                                      (handwritten_kind == ASSEMBLY_DIAGNOSTIC_UNKNOWN_INSTRUCTION &&
 13269:                                       (status == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_ENCODE_WRONG_OPERAND_COUNT ||
------
 13270:                                        status == BUSTER_X86_METADATA_ENCODE_OPERAND_MISMATCH))) &&
 13271:                                     status != BUSTER_X86_METADATA_ENCODE_UNKNOWN_MNEMONIC &&
 13272:                                     status != BUSTER_X86_METADATA_ENCODE_INVALID_INPUT &&
 13273:                                     (handwritten_kind != ASSEMBLY_DIAGNOSTIC_UNSUPPORTED_FEATURE ||
 13274:                                      builder->metadata_failure_typed_decorator_authoritative));
 13275:             if (metadata_precise)
 13276:             {
 13277:                 u32 length = statement.length > UINT32_MAX ? UINT32_MAX : (u32)statement.length;
 13278:                 assembly_x86_metadata_diagnostic(builder, status, line, column, length);
 13279:             }
 13280:             else if (handwritten_succeeded)
 13281:             {
 13282:                 assembly_instruction_parse_handwritten(builder, statement, line, column, offset, target, syntax);
 13283:             }
 13284:             else if (has_handwritten_diagnostic)
 13285:             {
 13286:                 assembly_diagnostic(builder, handwritten_diagnostic.kind, handwritten_diagnostic.line, handwritten_diagnostic.column,
 13287:                                     handwritten_diagnostic.length, handwritten_diagnostic.message);
 13288:             }
 13289:         }
 13290:     }
 13291: }

 13293: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL void assembly_source_parse(AssemblyBuilder* builder, String8 source, Target target, AssemblySyntax syntax)
------
 13502:     }
 13503:     builder->result.relocations[builder->result.relocation_count++] = (AssemblyRelocation){
 13504:         .addend = expression.addend + implicit_addend,
 13505:         .offset = offset,
 13506:         .symbol = expression.symbol,
 13507:         .kind = kind,
 13508:     };
 13509:     return true;
 13510: }

 13512: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_pc_value(AssemblyInstruction* instruction, u64 relocation_offset, u64 target,
 13513:                                                          s64 addend, s64* value)
 13514: {
 13515:     if (!instruction || !value || instruction->offset > (u64)INT64_MAX || relocation_offset > (u64)INT64_MAX ||
 13516:         instruction->offset > (u64)INT64_MAX - relocation_offset)
 13517:     {
 13518:         return false;
 13519:     }
 13520:     u64 place_unsigned = instruction->offset + relocation_offset;
 13521:     if (place_unsigned > (u64)INT64_MAX || target > (u64)INT64_MAX)
 13522:     {
 13523:         return false;
 13524:     }
 13525:     s64 target_value = (s64)target;
 13526:     s64 place_value = (s64)place_unsigned;
 13527:     if ((addend > 0 && target_value > INT64_MAX - addend) || (addend < 0 && target_value < INT64_MIN - addend))
------
 13531:     target_value += addend;
 13532:     if ((place_value > 0 && target_value < INT64_MIN + place_value) ||
 13533:         (place_value < 0 && target_value > INT64_MAX + place_value))
 13534:     {
 13535:         return false;
 13536:     }
 13537:     *value = target_value - place_value;
 13538:     return true;
 13539: }
 13540: 
 13541: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_relocation_kind(u8 metadata_kind, AssemblyRelocationKind* kind)
 13542: {
 13543:     if (kind)
 13544:     {
 13545:         switch ((BusterX86MetadataRelocationKind)metadata_kind)
 13546:         {
 13547:         case BUSTER_X86_METADATA_RELOCATION_ABSOLUTE8:
 13548:             *kind = ASSEMBLY_RELOCATION_X86_ABSOLUTE8;
 13549:             return true;
 13550:         case BUSTER_X86_METADATA_RELOCATION_ABSOLUTE16:
 13551:             *kind = ASSEMBLY_RELOCATION_X86_ABSOLUTE16;
 13552:             return true;
 13553:         case BUSTER_X86_METADATA_RELOCATION_ABSOLUTE32:
 13554:             *kind = ASSEMBLY_RELOCATION_X86_ABSOLUTE32;
 13555:             return true;
 13556:         case BUSTER_X86_METADATA_RELOCATION_ABSOLUTE64:
------
 13575:             *kind = ASSEMBLY_RELOCATION_X86_PC64;
 13576:             return true;
 13577:         case BUSTER_X86_METADATA_RELOCATION_KIND_COUNT:
 13578:             break;
 13579:         }
 13580:     }
 13581: 
 13582:     return false;
 13583: }
 13584: 
 13585: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool assembly_x86_metadata_local_relocation(AssemblyBuilder* builder, AssemblyInstruction* instruction,
 13586:                                                                  BusterX86MetadataRelocation relocation, u8* bytes)
 13587: {
 13588:     if (!builder || !instruction || !bytes || relocation.width == 0 || relocation.width > 8 ||
 13589:         relocation.offset > UINT32_MAX - relocation.width || relocation.offset + relocation.width > instruction->size)
 13590:     {
 13591:         return false;
 13592:     }
 13593:     u32 symbol_index = assembly_symbol_find(builder, relocation.symbol);
 13594:     if (symbol_index == UINT32_MAX)
 13595:     {
 13596:         return false;
 13597:     }
 13598:     AssemblySymbol symbol = builder->result.symbols[symbol_index];
 13599:     AssemblyRelocationKind kind = ASSEMBLY_RELOCATION_COUNT;
 13600:     if (!assembly_x86_metadata_relocation_kind(relocation.kind, &kind))
------
 13601:     {
 13602:         return false;
 13603:     }
 13604:     if (symbol.defined)
 13605:     {

  1829: // XED keeps the ordinary CMPXCHG/CMPXCHG16B memory rows as `nolock_prefix`
  1830: // aliases beside their lock-spelling rows.  The aliases are useful durable
  1831: // identities for machine recipes, but a LOCK byte is architectural only for
  1832: // these three memory forms.  Keep the exception explicit and shape-checked so
  1833: // a generic memory row can never gain an accidental atomic prefix.
  1834: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool buster_x86_metadata_emit_lock_alias_allowed(BusterX86MetadataForm form,
  1835:                                                                       BusterX86MetadataPatternSemantics pattern,
  1836:                                                                       bool has_memory)
  1837: {
  1838:     if (!has_memory || pattern.lock_control != 2 || pattern.mod_kind != BUSTER_X86_METADATA_PATTERN_MOD_MEMORY ||
  1839:         !pattern.has_modrm || form.coverage_class != BUSTER_X86_METADATA_COVERAGE_NORMALIZED)
  1840:         return false;
  1841:     return form.id == 9530 || form.id == 10278 || form.id == 10281;
  1842: }

   891: // A copy of `base` whose alignment is the one a typedef declarator asked for

  1418: 
  1419:    The declarator starts at the first punctuator that cannot be part of the
  1420:    specifiers -- the '*' of a pointer or the '(' of a parenthesized declarator
  1421:    -- or at the declared name, which is the first identifier whose declarator
  1422:    ends right after it. The tokens up to that end are its parameter list and
  1423:    its trailing attributes, and stepping over both is what puts the attribute
  1424:    of `void die(int) __attribute__((noreturn)), other(int);` past the boundary
  1425:    while `void __attribute__((noreturn)) die(int), other(int);` keeps its own
  1426:    before it. An identifier whose group is followed by anything else
  1427:    introduces a specifier instead -- `__typeof__(x) a, b;` and
  1428:    `_Atomic(int) a, b;` are the two that reach here -- so the scan continues
  1429:    past it. Attribute lists are stepped over wherever they appear, and so is a
  1430:    brace-enclosed aggregate or enum body, whose members are declarations with
  1431:    names of their own; neither can offer up an identifier as the name.
  1432: 
  1433:    A boundary landing earlier than the real one only drops a shared marker,
  1434:    which costs the unreachable code after a call and never emits any. Landing
  1435:    later is what would let one declarator's marker reach its siblings, so
  1436:    every step above stops at the first proof it has gone far enough. The
  1437:    alignment reader wants the same direction for a different reason: a dropped
  1438:    shared `aligned` underaligns every declarator of the list equally, while a
  1439:    late boundary silently overaligns whichever ones did not ask. */

  2126: // How a spelling of the atomic builtins names its operation, plus the two ways
  2127: // the GNU `__atomic_*` family differs from the `__c11_atomic_*` one it shares

  4243: BUSTER_C_INTERNAL IrTypeId c_ir_type_name(CIntegerIrBuilder* builder, u32 start, u32 end);

  4245: // May the parenthesized group opening at `open` spell a type name?  False
  4246: // only when the token after the `(` proves it cannot, without entering the
  4247: // query machine.  c_ir_type_name_prefix accepts a type name that begins with
  4248: // a type, qualifier, tag, typeof or _Atomic keyword, a decoration
  4249: // c_ir_primitive_type_kind steps over (an attribute, an asm label, a `[[`
  4250: // attribute) or an identifier naming a typedef, and nothing else, so:
  4251: // - a number, a string, a character and every punctuator but `[` is refused
  4252: //   outright;
  4253: // - an identifier the parser bound to a local, object, parameter,
  4254: //   enumerator or function is refused too: the prefix reads that binding
  4255: //   before any scope lookup, and none of them is a typedef.  Its spelling
  4256: //   must not be one of the keywords above, which
  4257: //   c_parse_type_name_start_word_token answers from the word bits (an
  4258: //   interned spelling outside the predefined range is no keyword; symbol 0
  4259: //   is unclassified and stays a candidate).
  4260: // An unbound identifier keeps the scope-chain lookup the probe does for it,
  4261: // and a typedef is the candidate the probe exists for.

  4695: 
  4696: /* Whether an atomic load or store of `type` -- the `_Atomic`-qualified type a
  4697:    place carries -- is one this toolchain can lower.
  4698: 
  4699:    An atomic aggregate is read and written as a single integer access of its
  4700:    *promoted* width, which is what c_atomic_promoted_layout pads the type up to
  4701:    (#731): a three-byte record is four bytes aligned four so that one four-byte
  4702:    access covers it.  The widths the backends implement that access at are one,
  4703:    two, four and eight bytes everywhere, plus sixteen on x86-64 when `cx16`
  4704:    gives them CMPXCHG16B and on AArch64 always, through the LDXP/STXP
  4705:    exclusive-pair loops -- see the IR_OPCODE_ATOMIC_LOAD and
  4706:    IR_OPCODE_ATOMIC_STORE branches in codegen.c, which are where the aggregate
  4707:    forms are emitted; the machine selectors refuse the aggregate shapes and fall
  4708:    back to those.  Anything wider would need a `libatomic` lock, and there is
  4709:    none here, so refusing in lowering is what turns an internal code generation
  4710:    failure into a diagnostic that names the type (#762).
  4711: 
------
  4712:    Only aggregates are asked about.  Every atomic scalar this frontend builds is
  4713:    already a lock-free width or is refused nearer its own kind -- an atomic wide
  4714:    float by the c_ir_type_contains_wide_float guards beside the callers -- so
  4715:    widening the question here would change behaviour that is not this one's. */
  4716: BUSTER_C_INTERNAL bool c_ir_atomic_aggregate_access_supported(CIntegerIrBuilder* builder, IrTypeId type)
  4717: {
  4718:     IrType* qualified = ir_type_from_id(&builder->program->types, type);
  4719:     IrType* unqualified = qualified && qualified->is_atomic ? ir_type_from_id(&builder->program->types, qualified->unqualified_type) : 0;
------
  4895:     IrType* place_type = ir_type_from_id(&builder->program->types, type);
  4896:     bool atomic = place_type && place_type->is_atomic;
  4897:     if (atomic && c_ir_type_contains_wide_float(builder->program, builder->wide_float_cache, type))
  4898:     {
  4899:         builder->failure_message = S8("C IR lowering does not yet support atomic wide floating-point loads");
  4900:         return IR_VALUE_ID_INVALID;
  4901:     }
  4902:     // A qualified place is read at its unqualified type: what an lvalue
  4903:     // conversion yields is the unqualified type of the object, and the access
  4904:     // itself stays volatile through `volatile_access` below, which reads the
  4905:     // place's own flag rather than the type. Doing this for `_Atomic` alone
  4906:     // left a `volatile` aggregate's value carrying the qualified copy of the
  4907:     // struct, and assigning it to the plain struct then had no conversion to
  4908:     // reach for -- the ladder in c_ir_emit_cast only spans the scalar kinds

  4917:         {

  4919:         }
  4920:     }

  5384:             return IR_VALUE_ID_INVALID;
  5385:         }
  5386:     }

  5391: /* The atomic type of an atomic-aggregate/operand pair, or an invalid id when
  5392:    these two types are not one.
  5393: 
  5394:    `_Atomic three` and `three` are two aggregates of two sizes -- the promotion
  5395:    pads the atomic one up to a lock-free width (#731) -- so neither ladder in
  5396:    c_ir_emit_cast has a conversion between them, and every position that builds
  5397:    the type reaches one: a parameter, a return, a call argument and a call
  5398:    result all carry the type the argument classification is made from, while
  5399:    every load and store between them carries the unqualified one (#786). */

  5405:     bool right_aggregate = right_value && (right_value->kind == IR_TYPE_STRUCT || right_value->kind == IR_TYPE_UNION);
  5406:     bool left_atomic = left_value && left_value->is_atomic && right_aggregate && left_value->unqualified_type.value == right.value;
  5407:     bool right_atomic = right_value && right_value->is_atomic && left_aggregate && right_value->unqualified_type.value == left.value;

 19175: // `E op= x` and `++E` on an `_Atomic` floating-point object (#821). C11
 19176: // 6.5.16.2p3 makes a compound assignment on an atomic type a read-modify-write,
 19177: // and the hardware has no floating-point atomic arithmetic, so the operation is
 19178: // a compare-exchange loop over the object's bit pattern -- which is what clang
 19179: // emits for the same source.
 19180: //
 19181: // The comparison the loop retries on has to be a *bitwise* one: comparing two
 19182: // floats with `==` never terminates once the object holds a NaN, and would
 19183: // terminate wrongly on a negative zero. The IR has no bitcast, so the integer
 19184: // view is taken through memory instead: the object's address is cast to a
 19185: // pointer of the same-width unsigned type, and a float-shaped scratch slot with
 19186: // an integer view of its own is what a value crosses the two formats through.
 19187: // Both views alias one object each, which is what makes the reinterpretation
 19188: // legal rather than a copy.
 19189: //
 19190: // `previous_out` receives the value the object held before the last successful

 19450: // `_Atomic ( type-name )` is the C11 atomic type specifier, which spells the
 19451: // type it applies to inside parentheses; `_Atomic` standing alone is the
 19452: // qualifier of the same name. Only the parenthesized form has to be told
 19453: // apart, and only here: the declaration path parses it through the type
 19454: // machine, but a cast reaches this type-name reader, where an unrecognized
 19455: // specifier left `(_Atomic(uint32_t) *)ptr` to be lowered as an expression.
 19456: // The IrType `_Atomic` builds over the typedef name `named_type`, or invalid
 19457: // when the name is not one whose alignment request `_Atomic` takes away.  A
 19458: // type name builds no `CType`, so it reaches the aligned alias's own IrType
 19459: // and would keep a request the same type spelled through a typedef of the
 19460: // atomic type had already dropped -- one type answering `_Alignof` 64 written
 19461: // inline and 4 written through the typedef (#726).

 19463: // too, so the two cannot answer differently, and the replacement is built from
 19464: // the alias's *unqualified* type exactly as the type-mapping pass builds the
 19465: // typedef spelling: c_ir_add_qualified_type's dedup then hands back the very

  1180: /* The layout `_Atomic T` has, given T's own.  Clang treats `_Atomic T` as
  1181:    *constructing* a type rather than qualifying one: a value it could operate on
  1182:    lock-free is padded up to the next power of two and aligned there, which is
  1183:    what makes `_Atomic struct { char a, b, c; }` four bytes aligned four where
  1184:    the record itself is three bytes aligned one, and what gives every atomic
  1185:    scalar an alignment equal to its size -- `_Atomic _Complex double` is
  1186:    sixteen-byte aligned where the complex type is eight.  The ceiling is the
  1187:    target's maximum lock-free width, `TargetDataLayout::atomic_max_width`: 128
  1188:    bits everywhere here but wasm64 and BPF, where it is 64.  A type wider than
  1189:    that keeps T's layout untouched, so the rule is not "round every aggregate
  1190:    up" -- Clang leaves a seventeen-byte record seventeen bytes aligned one --
  1191:    and a zero-sized aggregate still takes one byte, because an atomic operation
  1192:    on it has to touch something.  GCC pads nothing and raises the alignment only
  1193:    where the size is already a power of two; AGENTS.md names Clang the oracle
  1194:    and the divergence is an ABI one (#731).
  1195: 
------
  1196:    Both layout engines ask here, or a folded `sizeof` contradicts the object it
  1197:    measures the way `packed` and `aligned` already have the rule for:
  1198:    c_parse_type_layout below applies it to every atomic type it resolves, and
------
  1199:    c_ir_add_qualified_type in c_gen.c applies it to the copy the mapping pass
  1200:    builds.  Both read the width from the *preprocess* target's data layout --
------
  1201:    `program->data_layout` is that same record -- so the two cannot answer with
  1202:    different ceilings.  An **array keeps its own layout**: `_Atomic` of one is a
  1203:    constraint violation Clang and GCC both refuse, this frontend does not
  1204:    diagnose it in every position, and `c_ir_add_qualified_type` refuses to build
  1205:    the type at all -- so promoting here would leave the two engines answering
  1206:    16 and 12 for a type neither should have built.  Both callers below that can
  1207:    see one say so; the seed cannot, because no array resolves there. */

  6479:             seen_type = true;
  6480:         }
  6481:         else if (string_equal(spelling, S8("__int128")))
  6482:         {

  6484:             seen_type = true;
  6485:         }

  6487:         {

  6489:             seen_type = true;
  6490:         }

  6492:         {
  6493:             seen_unsigned = true;
  6494:             seen_type = true;
  6495:         }
  6496:         else if (string_equal(spelling, S8("long")))

  6956:         type->is_atomic = true;
  6957:     }
  6958:     return qualifier_bits != 0;

  6961: BUSTER_C_INTERNAL bool c_parse_type_qualifier_word_token(CPreprocessResult preprocess, CToken token, CType* type)
  6962: {

  6965: 
  6966: // `_Atomic` spells two different things with one keyword, and a `(` right
  6967: // after it is the only thing that tells them apart: `_Atomic ( T )` is a type
  6968: // specifier that constructs the atomic type from T, and `_Atomic` anywhere
  6969: // else is a qualifier that applies to whatever type the specifier run builds.
  6970: // The declaration-specifier scans have to ask before they decide whether the
  6971: // run continues, because stopping at every `_Atomic` leaves the qualifier
  6972: // spelling uncollected; c_ir_atomic_type_specifier_at in c_gen.c is the same
  6973: // question asked of a type name in an expression.
  6974: BUSTER_C_INTERNAL bool c_parse_atomic_type_specifier_at(CPreprocessResult preprocess, u32 index, u32 end)
  6975: {
  6976:     return index + 1 < end && preprocess.tokens[index].kind == C_TOKEN_IDENTIFIER &&
  6977:            (c_parse_word_bits_token(preprocess, preprocess.tokens[index]) & C_WORD_QUALIFIER_ATOMIC) != 0 &&
  6978:            c_token_is_punctuator(&preprocess.tokens[index + 1], C_PUNCTUATOR_LEFT_PARENTHESIS);
  6979: }
  6980:

  8617: 
  8618: // What a machineless operand's abstract declarator spells over its base type:
  8619: // the `*` chain with the qualifier run each level carries, then the array
  8620: // suffixes. `*index` lands past what was consumed, and an invalid base is
  8621: // handed straight back so a caller can tell a failed base type from a failed
  8622: // suffix.
  8623: //
  8624: // Two walks want exactly this. The sizeof/_Alignof operand walk below is the
  8625: // original one, and the `_Atomic ( T )` branch in c_parse_machineless_base_type
  8626: // is the second: the parenthesised operand of that specifier is a whole type
  8627: // name, so it needs the same suffixes the outer operand does (#784).
  8628: BUSTER_C_INTERNAL CTypeId c_parse_machineless_declarator_suffixes(CParseResult* result, CPreprocessResult preprocess, CTypeId type, u32* index, u32 end)
  8629: {
  8630:     if (type.value != C_ID_UNDERLYING_INVALID)
  8631:     {
  8632:         for (;;)
  8633:         {
  8634:             u32 pointer_start = *index;
  8635:             type = c_parse_pointer_chain(result, preprocess, type, index, end);
  8636:             CType qualifiers = {0};
  8637:             bool has_qualifier = false;
  8638:             while (*index < end && preprocess.tokens[*index].kind == C_TOKEN_IDENTIFIER &&
  8639:                    c_parse_type_qualifier_word_token(preprocess, preprocess.tokens[*index], &qualifiers))
  8640:             {
------
  8641:                 has_qualifier = true;
  8642:                 *index += 1;
  8643:             }
  8644:             // The run qualifies what the walk has built so far -- the base
  8645:             // type when no `*` preceded it, the pointer otherwise.  `_Atomic`
  8646:             // is the one qualifier that changes the layout the walk then asks
  8647:             // for, so dropping the run folded `sizeof(struct three _Atomic)`
  8648:             // to 3 where the same operand through the type-parse machine
  8649:             // folds 4.
  8650:             type = has_qualifier ? c_parse_add_qualified_type(result, type, qualifiers) : type;
  8651:             if (*index == pointer_start)
  8652:             {
  8653:                 break;
  8654:             }
  8655:         }
  8656:         type = c_parse_array_suffixes(result, preprocess, type, index, end);
  8657:     }
  8658:     return type;
  8659: }
  8660: 
------
  8661: // How many `_Atomic ( T )` specifiers may nest inside one machineless
  8662: // operand. The specifier's operand is a whole type name rather than a base
------
  8663: // type, so `_Atomic(_Atomic(int) *)` is legal C and the nesting follows the
  8664: // source -- which is why the levels go on an explicit stack below rather than
  8665: // on the C call stack. Past the stack the operand does not fold, which is
  8666: // where every depth of it stood before #784, and the machine-bearing path
  8667: // folds it at any depth either way.
  8668: #define C_PARSE_MACHINELESS_ATOMIC_LEVELS 8
  8669: 
  8670: // The base type of a machineless sizeof/_Alignof operand: a (qualified)
  8671: // typedef name, a named struct/union/enum tag, a primitive spelling, or an
  8672: // `_Atomic ( T )` specifier over any of those.
  8673: // `*index_out` lands past what was consumed. The type-parse machine is never
  8674: // entered, so this is callable from inside one of its steps — which is what
  8675: // lets a sizeof inside an array bound resolve while an enum body is being
  8676: // evaluated (the machine-bearing path uses c_parse_scalar_type instead).
------
  8677: BUSTER_C_INTERNAL CTypeId c_parse_machineless_base_type(CParseResult* result, CPreprocessResult preprocess, CScopeId scope, u32 start, u32 end,
  8678:                                                           u32* index_out)
------
  8679: {
  8680:     // The `_Atomic ( T )` levels the walk descended through, outermost first:
  8681:     // the qualifier run written ahead of each one, and where its `)` sits.
  8682:     CType atomic_qualifiers[C_PARSE_MACHINELESS_ATOMIC_LEVELS];
  8683:     u32 atomic_close[C_PARSE_MACHINELESS_ATOMIC_LEVELS];

  8685:     u32 base_start = start;
  8686:     u32 base_end = end;
  8687:     u32 index = start;
------
  8688:     CTypeId type = C_TYPE_ID_INVALID;
  8689:     bool refused = false;
  8690:     bool descending = true;
  8691:     while (descending)
  8692:     {
  8693:         descending = false;
  8694:         index = base_start;
  8695:         type = c_parse_qualified_typedef_type(result, preprocess, scope, base_start, base_end, &index);

  8696:         if (type.value == C_ID_UNDERLYING_INVALID)
  8697:         {
  8698:             // The qualifier run ahead of the tag keyword.
  8699:             // c_parse_qualified_typedef_type above collects the same run and
  8700:             // then discards it whenever the name it reaches is not a typedef,
  8701:             // which is every tag: `_Atomic struct three` is four bytes where
  8702:             // `struct three` is three, so an operand walk that drops the run
  8703:             // folds a size the type-parse machine does not (#761).  `const
  8704:             // struct S` and `volatile struct S` ride the same run and were
  8705:             // dropped with it -- the whole operand went unresolved, which
  8706:             // fails the enum constant that spells it rather than merely
  8707:             // mis-sizing it.
  8708:             CType tag_qualifiers = {0};
  8709:             bool has_tag_qualifier = false;
  8710:             u32 tag_index = c_parse_skip_attributes(preprocess, base_start, base_end);
  8711:             while (tag_index < base_end && preprocess.tokens[tag_index].kind == C_TOKEN_IDENTIFIER &&
  8712:                    !c_parse_atomic_type_specifier_at(preprocess, tag_index, base_end) &&

  8717:             }

  8719:             {
  8720:                 // Where the run stopped is the `_Atomic ( T )` specifier, and
  8721:                 // this walk had no branch for it at all: the typedef walk
  8722:                 // above steps over `_Atomic` as a qualifier and then finds a
  8723:                 // `(` where a name should be, and the tag branch below finds
  8724:                 // `_Atomic` where a keyword should be -- so the operand
  8725:                 // resolved to nothing whatever T was, and the enumerator that
  8726:                 // spelled it failed to fold, taking its enum type and every
  8727:                 // sibling enumerator with it (#784).  The parenthesised
  8728:                 // operand is a whole type name, so the walk descends into it
  8729:                 // and the loop below folds the levels back outward.
  8730:                 u32 close = c_parse_matching_delimiter(preprocess, tag_index + 1, base_end, C_PUNCTUATOR_LEFT_PARENTHESIS, C_PUNCTUATOR_RIGHT_PARENTHESIS);
  8731:                 if (close < base_end && tag_index + 2 < close && atomic_level_count < C_PARSE_MACHINELESS_ATOMIC_LEVELS)
  8732:                 {
  8733:                     atomic_qualifiers[atomic_level_count] = tag_qualifiers;
  8734:                     atomic_close[atomic_level_count] = close;
  8735:                     atomic_level_count += 1;

  8736:                     base_start = tag_index + 2;
  8737:                     base_end = close;
------
  8738:                     descending = true;
  8739:                 }

   620: // One type up to a `volatile` qualifier, in either order. The qualified copy
   621: // keeps the base's layout and kind, so a value of one is already a value of the
   622: // other; only the object's access rules differ, and those travel on the place
   623: // and on the access instead. See the declaration in model.h for why `_Atomic`
   624: // is not admitted here.
   625: bool ir_types_differ_only_in_volatile(IrTypeTable* table, IrTypeId left, IrTypeId right)
   626: {
   627:     IrType* left_type = ir_type_from_id(table, left);
   628:     IrType* right_type = ir_type_from_id(table, right);
   629:     bool result = false;
   630:     if (left_type && right_type && left.value != right.value && !left_type->is_atomic && !right_type->is_atomic)
   631:     {
   632:         IrTypeId left_base = left_type->is_volatile ? left_type->unqualified_type : left;
   633:         IrTypeId right_base = right_type->is_volatile ? right_type->unqualified_type : right;
   634:         result = left_base.value == right_base.value;
   635:     }
   636: 
   637:     return result;
   638: }

  2701: // **A qualifier decides nothing about how a value is passed.**  `_Atomic T`
  2702: // and `volatile T` are classified as the T they are built from, on every
  2703: // convention: the qualified copy carries T's kind and T's fields, `_Atomic`
  2704: // changes only the size and alignment (c_ir_add_qualified_type promotes them),
  2705: // and neither qualifier introduces a class of its own.  So a record holding an
  2706: // `_Atomic int` is the INTEGER eightbyte its `int` spelling is, a record
  2707: // holding an `_Atomic float` beside a plain one is still two floats -- SSE on
  2708: // System V, a homogeneous float aggregate on AAPCS64 -- and an atomic record
  2709: // passed by value is classified from the record.  The AAPCS64 homogeneity walk
  2710: // below is this step's only caller: System V already reads the leaf kind,
  2711: // which a qualified copy keeps.
  2712: //
  2713: // This is where the two references disagree and the oracle is not followed
  2714: // (#763).  Measured 2026-08-30 against Clang 22.1.8 and GCC 16.2.1:
  2715: //
  2716: //   - GCC classifies every one of these shapes exactly as the unqualified
------
  2717: //     spelling, on x86-64.  Its AArch64 answer was not measurable here (no
  2718: //     cross-GCC on the machine that measured this).
------
  2719: //   - Clang sends *any* record containing an atomic member, and any atomic
  2720: //     record, to MEMORY -- but only on System V x86-64.  The same Clang passes
  2721: //     them in registers on Win64, on AAPCS64 and on Darwin AArch64.
------
  2722: //   - Clang's AArch64 homogeneous-aggregate test declines separately: a member
------
  2723: //     of atomic float type stops the aggregate from being an HFA, so
  2724: //     `struct { _Atomic float a, b; }` rides X0 there where
  2725: //     `struct { float a, b; }` rides S0/S1.  A `volatile` member does not,
  2726: //     which is the tell that neither refusal is a rule about atomics.
  2727: //
  2728: // Both refusals are the same accident on Clang's side: `X86_64ABIInfo::classify`
  2729: // asks `Ty->getAs<RecordType>()` and `isHomogeneousAggregate` asks for a
  2730: // builtin type, an `AtomicType` is sugar over nothing, and each walk falls
  2731: // through to its "everything else" tail.  The psABI text states no such rule --
  2732: // an `_Atomic` object has the size and alignment of the type it is built from,
  2733: // and no class of its own -- and Clang contradicts itself across three of its
  2734: // own conventions, so it is read here as a fall-through rather than as an ABI
  2735: // position.  Following it would mean writing a rule neither document states,
  2736: // disagreeing with GCC everywhere and with Clang on every convention but one.
  2737: //
  2738: // The same reading fixed a divergence from *both* references: identity in the
  2739: // homogeneity walk was a type id, so `struct { float a; volatile float b; }`
------
  2740: // came out an integer pair where Clang and GCC both keep the two-register
  2741: // aggregate.
  2742: //
  2743: // The cost of the choice is recorded rather than hidden: a
  2744: // `struct { char c; _Atomic int v; }` argument sits in a register on this
  2745: // side of a System V x86-64 translation-unit boundary and in memory on
  2746: // Clang's.  `tests/basic_c_atomic_abi_shapes.h` and the callee/caller pair
  2747: // around it pin it, mixed with a real GCC there and with Clang on AArch64.
------
  2748: // One shape is missing from them and is decided by the rule alone: a *bare*
  2749: // atomic record passed by value, which fails code generation in every
  2750: // spelling today (#786) except the one that compiles only because it does not
  2751: // reach the type (#761).  The pair carries the wrapped shape instead --
  2752: // `struct { char c; _Atomic(struct pair) v; }` reaches the same walk.

  2754: {
  2755:     IrType* type = ir_type_from_id(table, id);
  2756:     // One step is the whole walk: c_ir_add_qualified_type refuses a qualified
  2757:     // base, so `volatile _Atomic T` is a single copy carrying both flags and
  2758:     // pointing straight at T.
  2759:     return type && (type->is_atomic || type->is_volatile) ? type->unqualified_type : id;

  2760: }

   506: // Whether two *different* types are one type up to a `volatile` qualifier: the
   507: // qualified copy a frontend builds for `volatile T` paired with `T` itself, in
   508: // either order. Two identical ids answer false, because every caller has
   509: // already answered that case. The qualifier belongs to the object, not to a
   510: // value read out of it or written into it, and an access carries its own
   511: // `volatile_access` flag, so a load, a store, and the frontend's value
   512: // conversions all let the difference pass. An `_Atomic` type is excluded: its
   513: // accesses are the atomic opcodes, which pair the qualified place with the
   514: // unqualified value themselves.

   602: static bool llvm_bc_add_ir_type(LlvmBcContext* context, u32 type_index)
   603: {
   604:     IrType* type = context->program->types.types + type_index;

   608:         // size, which is every atomic scalar: `_Atomic int` and `int` are one
   609:         // type. An atomic *aggregate* is wider than its operand, because the C
   610:         // type is padded up to the next power of two so that one lock-free
   611:         // access covers it (#731), and an LLVM type that stopped at the
   612:         // operand would size the object short of what `IrType::layout` -- and
   613:         // therefore the native object -- says. It gets a record of its own:
   614:         // the operand followed by a byte array of the padding, which is what
   615:         // Clang writes as `{ %struct.three, [1 x i8] }` (#767).

  2545: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL bool x86_64_metadata_test_source_reachability_skeleton(UnitTestArguments* arguments)
  2546: {
  2547:     Target target = {
  2548:         .cpu_arch = CPU_ARCH_X86_64,
  2549:         .cpu_model = CPU_MODEL_INTEL_DIAMOND_RAPIDS,
  2550:         .os = OPERATING_SYSTEM_LINUX,
  2551:     };
  2552:     static X86_64MetadataSourceReachabilityCase const cases[] = {
  2553:         // Snapshot form IDs are stable for the checked-in generated table.
  2554:         {.form_id = 9842, .source = S8_INITIALIZER("mov rax, rax\n")},
  2555:         {.form_id = 6939, .source = S8_INITIALIZER("vaddps zmm0, zmm0, zmm0, {rn-sae}\n")},
  2556:         {.form_id = 567, .source = S8_INITIALIZER("add rax, rax, rax\n")},
  2557:     };
  2558:     bool all_success = true;
  2559:     for (u32 index = 0; index < BUSTER_ARRAY_LENGTH(cases); index += 1)
  2560:     {
  2561:         X86_64MetadataSourceReachabilityResult reachability =
  2562:             x86_64_metadata_test_source_reachability_case(arguments->arena, target, cases[index]);
  2563:         all_success &= reachability.classification == X86_64_METADATA_SOURCE_REACHABILITY_SUCCESS;
  2564:         arguments->show(arguments, S8("X86_SOURCE_REACHABILITY form={u32} canonical={u32} direct={u32} operands={u32} source={u32} match={u32} diag={u32} direct_bytes={u32}:{u32} source_bytes={u32}:{u32} mismatch={u32}:{u32}:{u32} class={u32}\n"),
  2565:                         reachability.form_id, reachability.canonical_query, reachability.direct_emission,
  2566:                         reachability.physical_operand_count, reachability.source_encoded, reachability.bytes_match,
  2567:                         reachability.diagnostic_kind, reachability.direct_byte_count, reachability.direct_first_byte,
  2568:                         reachability.source_byte_count, reachability.source_first_byte, reachability.mismatch_index,
  2569:                         reachability.mismatch_direct_byte, reachability.mismatch_source_byte, reachability.classification);
  2570:     }
