  1029:     // GNU clzll/ctzll spellings normally describe 64-bit operands; Buster's
  1030:     // frontend deliberately preserves an i128 operand width, so this test

  1032:     // runtime fixture exercises the resulting pair stores separately.

  1038:         "}\n");

  1985:                                   // The live-range-splitting shape: twenty-four values live from before
  1986:                                   // a call-free hot loop across a later call loop — past both
  1987:                                   // targets' register files. The calls foreclose the

===== src/buster/tests/compiler/llvm/bitcode_test.c: hits [4] =====
     1: #include <buster/tests/compiler/llvm/bitcode_test.h>
     2: #if BUSTER_INCLUDE_TESTS
     3: 
     4: /* One module holding a three-byte record and an `_Atomic` copy of it whose
     5:    promoted size is `atomic_size`, emitted for its type table alone: the walk in
     6:    llvm_bc_build_types visits every type in the program, so no global has to
     7:    name them for the records to be written.
     8: 
     9:    `atomic_size` 4 is what the frontend builds -- an atomic type is padded up to
    10:    the next power of two (#731) -- and 3 is the atomic-scalar shape, where the
    11:    operand's size already covers the object. The two answer differently: the
    12:    padded one needs a record of its own, the operand followed by a byte array
    13:    (#767), and the unpadded one is its operand's type exactly. */
    14: BUSTER_GLOBAL_LOCAL LlvmBitcodeArtifact llvm_bitcode_test_atomic_record(Arena* arena, u64 atomic_size, bool include_atomic)
    15: {
    16:     IrField* fields = arena_allocate(arena, IrField, 3);
    17:     for (u32 index = 0; index < 3; index += 1)
    18:     {
    19:         fields[index] = (IrField){.type = {.value = 1}, .offset = index};
------

===== tests/basic_c_aarch64_i128.c: hits [10, 11] =====
     1: /*
     2:  * AArch64 canonical i128 arithmetic fixture.
     3:  *
     4:  * The helpers write through pointers deliberately.  The current AArch64
     5:  * caller path does not promise a direct i128 return shape yet; storing both
     6:  * halves still exercises the canonical operation and makes stale high-half
     7:  * results visible to a runtime oracle when this fixture is linked under
     8:  * qemu-aarch64 (or on an AArch64 host).
     9:  */
    10: typedef unsigned __int128 U128;
    11: typedef __int128 S128;
    12: typedef unsigned int U32;
    13: typedef unsigned long long U64;
    14: 
    15: static void unsigned_divide(U128 dividend, U128 divisor, U128 *quotient, U128 *remainder)
    16: {
    17:     *quotient = dividend / divisor;
    18:     *remainder = dividend % divisor;
    19: }
    20: 
    21: static void signed_divide(S128 dividend, S128 divisor, S128 *quotient, S128 *remainder)
    22: {
    23:     *quotient = dividend / divisor;
    24:     *remainder = dividend % divisor;
    25: }

===== tests/basic_c_aarch64_i128_abi.c: hits [1, 14, 17, 25, 32, 39, 46, 51, 53, 67, 72, 76, 80, 88] =====
     1: // Focused AAPCS64 __int128 boundary fixture.  The pair in a bare integer-128
     2: // value is an INTEGER pair, but the pair itself has sixteen-byte alignment:
     3: // after an odd X-register cursor it rounds to the next even register, and
     4: // after the register file closes its stack image starts at an even eightbyte.
     5: // The functions are deliberately call boundaries rather than only arithmetic
     6: // bodies so a caller/callee disagreement cannot hide in one compiler's frame
     7: // representation.  This file has no hosted dependencies and therefore also
     8: // runs as a static AArch64 image under qemu-aarch64 when the emulator exists.
     9: typedef unsigned char u8;
    10: typedef unsigned long long u64;
    11: 
    12: typedef struct
    13: {
    14:     __int128 value;
    15: } I128Wrapper;
    16: 
------
    17: static int check_i128(__int128 value, u64 low, u64 high)
    18: {
    19:     return (u64)value == low && (u64)(value >> 64) == high;
    20: }
    21: 
    22: // One consumed GPR leaves x1, so C.8 rounds the pair to x2:x3.  The trailing
    23: // scalar catches a callee that starts the pair at x1 or fails to advance two
    24: // registers after it.
    25: static int after_one(int tag, __int128 value, int tail)
    26: {
    27:     return tag == 11 && check_i128(value, 0x0123456789abcdefULL, 0x1122334455667788ULL) && tail == 22;
    28: }
    29: 
------
    30: // Six consumed GPRs leave x6, already even, so the pair fits exactly in
    31: // x6:x7.  This is the non-rounding register-pair case.
    32: static int after_six(int a, int b, int c, int d, int e, int f, __int128 value)
------
    33: {
    34:     return a == 1 && b == 2 && c == 3 && d == 4 && e == 5 && f == 6 && check_i128(value, 7, 8);
    35: }
    36: 
    37: // Seven consumed GPRs leave only x7.  The pair spills at the stack base and
    38: // closes the X file, so the following scalar must also be stacked.
    39: static int after_seven(int a, int b, int c, int d, int e, int f, int g, __int128 value, int tail)
    40: {
------
    41:     return a == 1 && b == 2 && c == 3 && d == 4 && e == 5 && f == 6 && g == 7 && check_i128(value, 9, 10) && tail == 11;
    42: }
    43: 
    44: // The ninth scalar occupies stack eightbyte zero.  A sixteen-byte pair that
    45: // follows it must skip eightbyte one and begin at NSAA+16.
    46: static int after_stacked_scalar(int a, int b, int c, int d, int e, int f, int g, int h, int i, __int128 value)
    47: {
------
    48:     return a == 1 && b == 2 && c == 3 && d == 4 && e == 5 && f == 6 && g == 7 && h == 8 && i == 9 && check_i128(value, 12, 13);
    49: }
    50: 
    51: static __int128 make_i128(void)

    53:     return ((__int128)0x8899aabbccddeeffULL << 64) | (__int128)0x0123456789abcdefULL;

===== tests/basic_c_atomic_abi_shapes.h: hits [8, 22, 28, 29, 35, 44, 58, 71, 87, 95] =====
     1: // The shapes the atomic-argument pair passes across the compiler boundary.
     2: // Both halves include this file and each half may be built by a different
     3: // compiler, so a disagreement about which register -- or whether a register at
     4: // all -- carries a record holding an atomic member becomes a wrong answer
     5: // rather than a wrong-looking disassembly.
     6: //
     7: // This pair pins a decision rather than a bug (#763).  A qualifier decides
     8: // nothing about how a value is passed here: `_Atomic T` and `volatile T` are
     9: // classified as the T they are built from, so every record below rides exactly
    10: // the registers its unqualified spelling rides.  GCC 16.2.1 answers the same
    11: // on x86-64.  Clang 22.1.8 does not, in two separate places: on System V
    12: // x86-64 it sends any record containing an atomic member -- and any atomic
    13: // record -- to memory, and on AArch64 it declines to call an aggregate with an
    14: // atomic floating member homogeneous.  It does neither on Win64 or Darwin
    15: // AArch64, and does neither for `volatile`, which is why both are read as
    16: // fall-throughs in its walks rather than as ABI positions.  See
    17: // ir_abi_unqualified_type in ir.c for the whole reasoning and the measurements.
    18: //
    19: // Every shape here has the same size and alignment under Clang, GCC and this
    20: // compiler (measured 2026-08-30), so only the argument class is under test: a
    21: // half that laid one out differently would fail for a reason this pair is not
    22: // about.  That is why the promoted-layout shapes -- `_Atomic` of a three-byte
    23: // record and friends, where Clang pads and GCC does not (#731) -- stay in
------
    24: // tests/basic_c_packed_layout.c and out of any cross-linked header.
    25: #ifndef BASIC_C_ATOMIC_ABI_SHAPES_H
    26: #define BASIC_C_ATOMIC_ABI_SHAPES_H
    27: 
    28: // One INTEGER eightbyte: eight bytes holding a `char` and an `_Atomic int`.
    29: // The `_Atomic int` alone is what Clang's System V walk trips over, so this is
    30: // the smallest shape the decision is visible in -- it rides `rdi`/`x0` here
    31: // and GCC's `edi`, and arrives on the stack from a Clang-built System V caller.
    32: struct atomic_scalar_member
    33: {
    34:     char c;
    35:     _Atomic int v;
    36: };
    37: 
------
    38: // Two INTEGER eightbytes, so the pair also pins the second half of the
    39: // register cursor: a memory classification moves both halves at once, and a
    40: // half that classified only the atomic eightbyte would place the second one
    41: // against a different register.
    42: struct atomic_pair_member
    43: {
------
    44:     _Atomic long long a;
------
    45:     long long b;
    46: };
    47: 
    48: // An atomic *aggregate* member rather than an atomic scalar one, so the walk
    49: // has to go through the atomic type into the record it is built from: this is
    50: // two INTEGER eightbytes because `struct atomic_int_pair` is, and Clang sends
------
    51: // the whole thing to memory on System V for the same reason it sends the
    52: // scalar shape above.  Reading the member needs an atomic load of the
    53: // aggregate, which is the lowering #762 added -- a half without it does not
    54: // compile this at all.
    55: //
    56: // A *bare* atomic record passed by value is the same rule and is not here: an
    57: // atomic aggregate parameter fails code generation in every spelling today
    58: // (#786), and the one spelling that does compile -- `_Atomic` written in front
    59: // of the tag -- compiles because it is not reaching the type at all (#761), so
------
    60: // a fixture written that way would pass while testing nothing.  It belongs in
    61: // this pair once either lands.
    62: struct atomic_int_pair
    63: {
    64:     int a;
    65:     int b;
    66: };
    67: 
    68: struct atomic_record_member
    69: {
    70:     char c;
    71:     _Atomic(struct atomic_int_pair) v;
    72: };
    73: 
------
    74: // A `volatile` floating member, which neither reference lets disturb the
    75: // class: this is the two-register homogeneous float aggregate on AArch64 and
    76: // the single SSE eightbyte on System V that `struct { float a, b; }` is.  It
    77: // is in the pair because it is the same element-identity question the atomic
    78: // spelling below asks and the one both references answer our way, so it pins
    79: // the mechanism against a compiler that is not this one.
    80: struct volatile_float_member
    81: {
    82:     float a;
    83:     volatile float b;
    84: };
    85: 
    86: #ifndef ATOMIC_ABI_REFERENCE_DECLINES_ATOMIC_HFA
------
    87: // The same shape with `_Atomic` in place of `volatile`, which is the shape the
    88: // decision is about on the floating side.  GCC classifies it SSE on x86-64
    89: // exactly as the plain spelling; Clang declines the homogeneous aggregate on
    90: // AArch64, so the lane that links against Clang there defines the macro above
    91: // and leaves this shape out.  Every other lane carries it.
    92: struct atomic_float_member
    93: {
    94:     float a;
    95:     _Atomic float b;
    96: };
    97: #endif
    98: 
    99: // The by-value directions.  Each shape crosses first as an argument in the
   100: // leading register, then after enough scalars to exhaust the cheap end of the
   101: // register file -- five integers leave one general-purpose argument register
   102: // on both conventions, and five floats leave the sixth vector register -- so a
------
   103: // half that sent the record to memory instead disagrees about the last
   104: // argument's home rather than about the first one's.  The `make` direction
   105: // pins the result class the same way; there is no atomic *result* to pin
   106: // beside it, because a function's return type drops its qualifiers.
   107: extern int atomic_abi_scalar_member_sum(struct atomic_scalar_member x);

===== tests/basic_c_atomic_aggregate.c: hits [1, 12, 13, 20, 42, 51, 52, 53, 54, 55, 65, 77, 184, 203, 204, 218, 224, 226, 230, 236, 238, 242, 248, 250, 254, 261, 263, 307, 322, 324] =====
     1: /* `_Atomic` over a struct or union: the object is padded up to the next power
     2:    of two (#731) so that one integer access of that width covers it, and the
     3:    load and the store are that access (#762).  Every expected byte here is
     4:    baked in rather than computed, so the fixture pins the *representation* and
     5:    not merely self-consistency: it was read out of Clang, which zeroes an
     6:    atomic aggregate's padding through the temporary it copies the value into.
     7: 
     8:    `signed char` is spelled out because plain `char` is unsigned on AArch64,
     9:    which would make the baked answers disagree with themselves across targets.
    10: 
    11:    Widths one through eight run everywhere.  Sixteen is x86-64 only: it is the
    12:    CMPXCHG16B pair, and AArch64 has no 128-bit lock-free access here (nor for
    13:    `_Atomic __int128`), where the frontend refuses it with a diagnostic
    14:    instead. */

    82:     int equal = 1;

    84:     {
    85:         if (actual[index] != expected[index])
    86:         {
    87:             equal = 0;
    88:         }
    89:     }
    90:     return equal;
    91: }

   174: {

   193: /* Passing and returning one (#786).  The type an argument is classified from
   194:    is the promoted one, so what travels is four bytes where the record is three
   195:    and the padding byte travels with it: the value reaches the call through an
   196:    object of the atomic type, which is where its zero comes from and is where
   197:    Clang's comes from too.  Nothing reads a member out of the atomic object
   198:    itself -- an atomic aggregate is copied whole and read through the copy,
   199:    which is also what Clang's -Watomic-access asks for.

===== tests/basic_c_atomic_bitcode.c: hits [3, 20, 33, 34, 42, 54] =====
     1: /* The shapes an atomic aggregate reaches the LLVM bitcode writer through.
     2: 
     3:    `_Atomic T` is a type built from T rather than a qualified T: it is padded up
     4:    to the next power of two so that one lock-free access covers it (#731), so
     5:    its LLVM type has to be the operand's wrapped with that padding -- what Clang
     6:    writes as `{ %struct.three, [1 x i8] }` -- and not the operand's own, which
     7:    would size the object short of what the native object already gives it
     8:    (#767).
     9: 
    10:    Declarations only, in the three positions the writer builds a type for: a
    11:    file-scope object, an aggregate member, and a block-scope object. An atomic
    12:    aggregate can be loaded and stored as of #762, but the type is the same
    13:    either way and keeping the accesses out leaves this fixture readable by a
    14:    compiler that only has the declaration half. */
    15: 
    16: typedef struct
    17: {
    18:     signed char a, b, c;
------
    19: } three;
    20: typedef _Atomic three atomic_three;
    21:

    23: {
    24:     int a, b, c;
    25: } twelve;
    26: 
    27: typedef union
    28: {
    29:     signed char bytes[3];
    30: } narrow_union;

    34: _Atomic narrow_union file_scope_union;
    35:

    36: /* No padding: the operand's size already covers the object, so this one stays
    37:    its operand's LLVM type. */

    41: } eight;

    58: }

===== tests/basic_c_atomic_float_update.c: hits [1, 16, 17, 18] =====
     1: // Compound assignment and increment on an `_Atomic` floating-point object
     2: // (issue #821). Both were refused, while the same operators on atomic integers
     3: // worked and a plain load/modify/store of an atomic float worked -- so the gap
     4: // was the read-modify-write, not the type.
     5: //
     6: // C11 6.5.16.2p3 makes a compound assignment on an atomic type one
     7: // read-modify-write, and no hardware has floating-point atomic arithmetic, so
     8: // this lowers to a compare-exchange loop over the object's bit pattern, which
     9: // is what clang emits for the same source.
    10: //
    11: // The comparison the loop retries on is bitwise, and the last two blocks are
    12: // why: a float comparison never settles once the object holds a NaN, and calls
    13: // a negative zero equal to a positive one. A run that hangs is the regression
    14: // this file catches.

===== tests/basic_c_atomic_specifier.c: hits [2, 3, 5, 10, 15, 18, 28, 29] =====
     1: // `quickjs-libc.c` reaches an atomic through a cast:
     2: // `atomic_fetch_add((_Atomic(uint32_t) *)ptr, v)`.  The declaration path
     3: // already parsed `_Atomic ( type-name )`, but the type-name reader a cast
     4: // goes through did not, so the cast was lowered as an expression and
     5: // `_Atomic` reached lowering as an unbound identifier.  The atomicity also
     6: // has to reach the IR type: an atomic builtin reads it off the place its
     7: // pointer argument dereferences to.

===== tests/basic_c_gnu_atomic_builtins.c: hits [9, 16, 19, 81, 90] =====
     1: // The GNU `__atomic_*` builtin family (issue #829). The frontend carried the
     2: // `__c11_atomic_*` family only, so CPython's configure probe for
     3: // HAVE_BUILTIN_ATOMIC failed and pyconfig.h diverged from the Clang reference;
     4: // glibc's headers and most Linux userland reach for this spelling too.
     5: //
     6: // The two families share every operation and lower to the same IR. Three things
     7: // differ, and each has a check here:
     8: //
     9: //   - the first argument is an ordinary pointer, not a pointer to an `_Atomic`
    10: //     object;
    11: //   - `__atomic_add_fetch` and its four siblings answer the updated value where
    12: //     `__atomic_fetch_add` answers the previous one;
    13: //   - arithmetic on a pointer object is in *bytes*, not elements. Both clang
    14: //     and gcc leave `p` unmoved by `__atomic_add_fetch(&p, 2, ...)` on an
    15: //     `int*` -- measured 2026-08-30 -- where the C11 spelling on an
    16: //     `_Atomic(int*)` advances it by two elements. tests/basic_c_atomic.c is
    17: //     the C11 side of that pair.
    18: //
    19: // `__atomic_compare_exchange_n` carries the `weak` flag the C11 name spells;
    20: // a strong exchange satisfies a weak request, so the flag is read and
    21: // discarded and both spellings lower to the strong form.
    22:

    24: static char flag;

    48:     (_Bool)2, (_Bool)256, (_Bool)-2, (_Bool)0.5, (_Bool)-0.5,
    49:     (_Bool)((unsigned __int128)1 << 100), (_Bool)&scalar,
    50:     (_Bool)0, (_Bool)0.0, (_Bool)-0.0, (_Bool)(void *)0,
    51: };
------
    52: static const _Bool *observed_truth = truth;
    53: 
    54: static int check_constant_promotions(void)
    55: {
    56:     int failures = 0;
    57:     const long long expected[] = {-1, 1, -1, -2, 2, -1, -2, -2, -2, 2147483648LL};
    58:     for (unsigned i = 0; i < sizeof(expected) / sizeof(expected[0]); ++i)
    59:         failures += observed_promoted[i] != expected[i];
    60:     for (unsigned i = 0; i < sizeof(truth) / sizeof(truth[0]); ++i)
    61:         failures += observed_truth[i] != (i < 7);
    62:     return failures;
    63: }

   712: // reference compilers disagree: Clang treats `_Atomic T` as constructing a type
   713: // rather than qualifying one, so the alias's request does not travel into it,
   714: // while GCC keeps it.  Clang is the oracle here, and it was already the answer

   789: // The other half of what `_Atomic` constructs: Clang pads an atomic aggregate
   790: // up to the next power of two and aligns it there, so a value it can operate
   791: // on lock-free has an instruction that reaches it -- there is no three-byte
------
   792: // atomic instruction and there is a four-byte one.  The ceiling is the
   793: // target's maximum lock-free width, 128 bits here, and above it the type keeps
   794: // the aggregate's own layout: `_Atomic` of a seventeen-byte record is
   795: // seventeen bytes aligned one in Clang too, so the rule is not "round every
   796: // aggregate up" (#731).  GCC pads nothing -- it answers 3 aligned 1 for the
   797: // first shape below and 12 aligned 4 for `atomic_twelve` -- and AGENTS.md
   798: // names Clang the oracle, which is what these numbers are.  Like the shapes
------
   799: // above they stay out of basic_c_packed_layout_shapes.h, whose other half is

   800: // whichever host compiler the platform has.

===== tests/basic_c_win64_narrow_abi.c: hits [3, 15, 143, 148, 150, 155, 207, 212, 217, 218] =====
     1: // The Win64 call shapes that clang picks for values narrower than one vector
     2: // register, crossing real call boundaries in both directions. MSVC has no
     3: // generic vector extension and no __int128, so clang's answers are the
     4: // de-facto ABI here; each was measured against clang 22 with
     5: // --target=x86_64-pc-windows-msvc before being implemented:
     6: //
     7: //   - A single-lane vector travels exactly like its scalar element: integer
     8: //     lanes ride the positional GPR and come back in RAX, float lanes ride
     9: //     the positional XMM register and come back in XMM0. Buster used to pass
    10: //     every Win64 vector by reference, so these four families disagreed with
    11: //     clang in both directions.
    12: //   - A multi-lane vector under eight bytes is an indirect argument and a
    13: //     direct XMM0 result. The result half is what buster used to refuse: it
    14: //     returned anything narrower than eight bytes by reference.
    15: //   - __int128 is an indirect argument and an XMM0 result. Buster used to
    16: //     return it through the caller's hidden pointer.
    17: //
    18: // The fixture is deliberately callee-in-another-function: a round trip
------
    19: // through one identity function cannot tell an argument-side disagreement
    20: // from a return-side one, so every family checks the two halves separately.
    21: // tests/c_abi_* pairs the same shapes against clang objects for real; this
    22: // fixture is the in-tree regression that runs on every platform.
    23: typedef unsigned char u8;
    24: typedef unsigned short u16;
    25: typedef unsigned int u32;
    26: typedef unsigned long long u64;
    27: 
    28: typedef u8 Vector1U8 __attribute__((vector_size(1)));
    29: typedef u16 Vector1U16 __attribute__((vector_size(2)));
    30: typedef u32 Vector1U32 __attribute__((vector_size(4)));
------
   133: static int take_vector_2_f32(Vector2F32 value, int tag)
   134: {
   135:     return value[0] == 1.5f && value[1] == 2.5f && tag == 20;
   136: }
   137: 
   138: static Vector2F32 make_vector_2_f32(void)
   139: {
   140:     return (Vector2F32){3.5f, 4.5f};
   141: }
   142: 
   143: static int take_int128(__int128 value, int tag)
   144: {
   145:     return (u64)value == 0x0123456789abcdefull && (u64)(value >> 64) == 0x1122334455667788ull && tag == 21;
   146: }
   147: 
   148: static __int128 make_int128(void)
   149: {
   150:     return ((__int128)0x99aabbccddeeff00ull << 64) | (__int128)0x0f1e2d3c4b5a6978ull;
   151: }
   152: 
   153: // Two 128-bit arguments in a row: the second one is what catches a callee
   154: // that read its indirect reference from the wrong register.
   155: static int take_int128_pair(__int128 first, __int128 second, int tag)
   156: {
   157:     return (u64)first == 1 && (u64)(first >> 64) == 2 && (u64)second == 3 && (u64)(second >> 64) == 4 && tag == 22;
   158: }

===== tests/basic_c_x86_64_i128_stack_abi.c: hits [4, 8, 17, 26, 34, 42, 54, 63, 67, 71] =====
     1: // System V x86-64 places a sixteen-byte-aligned stack argument at a
     2: // sixteen-aligned offset in the argument area, not immediately after the
     3: // eightbyte before it.  The machine placement used to pack the cursor tight,
     4: // so a machine-selected callee read a stacked __int128 one eightbyte early
     5: // while the canonical caller (and clang) had padded — a disagreement only a
     6: // run can see, and only when the callee itself stays inside the machine
     7: // subset.  Every callee here therefore forwards its halves to the one
     8: // checker that owns the __int128 shifts: the shifts are what push a function
     9: // to the canonical fallback, and a callee that fell back would silently stop
    10: // covering the machine placement.  basic_c_x86_64_i128_stack_abi in
    11: // driver_test.c pins that census.  This file has no hosted dependencies and
    12: // the same shapes hold on AArch64, whose placement rounds by C.8/C.12.
    13: typedef unsigned long long u64;
    14: 
    15: typedef struct I128Box
    16: {
    17:     __int128 value;
    18: } I128Box;
    19: 
------
    20: typedef struct AlignedPair
    21: {
    22:     _Alignas(16) long low;
    23:     long high;
------
    24: } AlignedPair;
    25: 
    26: static int check_halves(__int128 value, u64 low, u64 high)
    27: {
    28:     return (u64)value == low && (u64)(value >> 64) == high;
    29: }
    30: 
    31: // Seven integer arguments leave an odd stack cursor: g takes eightbyte zero,
    32: // so the sixteen-aligned pair must skip eightbyte one and start at sixteen.
------
    33: // The trailing scalar catches a callee that never counted the padding.
    34: static int bare_after_seven(int a, int b, int c, int d, int e, int f, int g, __int128 value, int tail)
    35: {
    36:     return a == 1 && b == 2 && c == 3 && d == 4 && e == 5 && f == 6 && g == 7 && check_halves(value, 9, 10) && tail == 11;
    37: }
    38: 
    39: // Eight integer arguments leave an even cursor: g and h take eightbytes zero
    40: // and one, the pair starts at sixteen with no padding to insert.  This is the
    41: // tight case, and the caller must not invent a gap where none belongs.
------
    42: static int bare_after_eight(int a, int b, int c, int d, int e, int f, int g, int h, __int128 value, int tail)
    43: {
    44:     return a == 1 && b == 2 && c == 3 && d == 4 && e == 5 && f == 6 && g == 7 && h == 8 && check_halves(value, 12, 13) && tail == 14;
    45: }
    46: 
    47: // The same rounding through the aggregate shape: a one-member wrapper keeps
    48: // the sixteen-byte alignment of the pair it carries.
    49: static int boxed_after_seven(int a, int b, int c, int d, int e, int f, int g, I128Box box, int tail)
------
    50: {
    51:     return a == 1 && b == 2 && c == 3 && d == 4 && e == 5 && f == 6 && g == 7 && check_halves(box.value, 15, 16) && tail == 17;
    52: }
    53: 
    54: // A sixteen-aligned aggregate with no __int128 inside: _Alignas raises the
    55: // slot alignment the same way the pair's own type does.
    56: static int aligned_after_seven(int a, int b, int c, int d, int e, int f, int g, AlignedPair pair, int tail)
    57: {
------
    58:     return a == 1 && b == 2 && c == 3 && d == 4 && e == 5 && f == 6 && g == 7 && pair.low == 18 && pair.high == 19 && tail == 20;
    59: }
    60: 
    61: int main(void)
    62: {
    63:     if (!bare_after_seven(1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, ((__int128)10 << 64) | 9, 11))
    64:     {
    65:         return 1;
    66:     }
    67:     if (!bare_after_eight(1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, ((__int128)13 << 64) | 12, 14))
    68:     {
    69:         return 2;
------
    70:     }
    71:     I128Box box = {.value = ((__int128)16 << 64) | 15};
    72:     if (!boxed_after_seven(1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, box, 17))
    73:     {
    74:         return 3;
    75:     }
    76:     AlignedPair pair = {.low = 18, .high = 19};
    77:     if (!aligned_after_seven(1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, pair, 20))
    78:     {
------
    79:         return 4;
    80:     }
    81:     return 0;
    82: }

===== tests/c_abi.h: hits [43] =====
    33: // like GCC's and clang's, bare returns stay in XMM0) and the Win64 narrow
    34: // fix below, the gated matrix passes cross-compiler against clang in both
    35: // directions, so pairings no longer need it; it remains available for
    36: // targets whose vector ABI is still being brought up.
    37: //
    38: // Win64 needed its own answers for everything narrower than one vector
    39: // register, because MSVC has no generic vector extension and clang's choices
    40: // are therefore the de-facto ABI: a single-lane vector travels like its
    41: // scalar element (integer lanes in the positional GPR, float lanes in the
    42: // positional XMM register), and a multi-lane vector under eight bytes is an
    43: // indirect argument with a direct XMM0 result. __int128 answers the same way
    44: // -- indirect argument, XMM0 result. Buster passed all of these by reference
    45: // in both directions before, which agreed with itself and with nothing else.
    46: 
    47: #ifndef C_ABI_FULL
    48: // Non-power-of-two lane counts are rejected as function parameter and return
    49: // types.
    50: #define ZIG_NO_NON_POW2_VECTORS
    51: // Vectors wider than 64 bytes cross the boundary on Win64 x86-64 only:
    52: // that convention legalizes them the way clang and MSVC do (one indirect
    53: // reference per register-sized piece for an argument, up to four direct
    54: // registers or the hidden pointer for a result), while every other
    55: // convention still rejects the signature types. Target-keyed like the
    56: // F128/INT128_SHIFTS gates below, so both sides agree whichever compiler
    57: // builds them.
    58: #if !(defined(_WIN32) && defined(__x86_64__))
------

 16447: void c_small_packed_struct(uint8_t x) {
 16448:     assert_or_panic(((x >> 0) & 0x3) == 0);

 16460: }

 16465: }
